<section class="v25-section" id="v25Method">
 <div class="v25-section-head"><div><div class="v25-eyebrow">THE RJ LEARNING JOURNEY</div><h2>Learn it. Use it. Create with it. Use it for real. Master it.</h2><p>One topic can now become a connected journey instead of five unrelated question pages.</p></div></div>
 <div class="v25-journey">
  <div class="v25-step"><b>01 · LEARN</b><h3>Understand</h3><p>Explanation, examples and noticing.</p></div>
  <div class="v25-step"><b>02 · USE</b><h3>Controlled practice</h3><p>Build, match, repair and choose.</p></div>
  <div class="v25-step"><b>03 · CREATE</b><h3>Produce English</h3><p>Write, build or explain something yourself.</p></div>
  <div class="v25-step"><b>04 · MISSION</b><h3>Use it in context</h3><p>Apply the language in a realistic situation.</p></div>
  <div class="v25-step"><b>05 · MASTER</b><h3>Prove it</h3><p>Complete a mixed challenge without step-by-step help.</p></div>
 </div>
</section>

Use it for a connection between past and present. For introduces a duration; since introduces a starting point. Finished past times such as yesterday normally go with past simple.
